# Flavoring

<section class="hmi-quick-read" id="flavoring-quick-read" data-hmi-quick-read aria-labelledby="flavoring-quick-read-label"><div class="hmi-quick-read-label" id="flavoring-quick-read-label">Quick read</div>

Flavoring in the HMI library has 69 reported results for lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, antimony, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V and Mo from 7 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Angelon-Gaetz KA, Klaus C, Chaudhry EA, Bean DK · 2018</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Lead in Spices, Herbal Remedies, and Ceremonial Powders Sampled from Home Investigations for Children with Elevated Blood Lead Levels — North Carolina, 2011–2018</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? TEP (FY2009-FY2024)</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">FDA analytical results table</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/fda2024-toxic-elements-baby-food-compliance-2009-2024">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/dokubo2023-nigeria-seasoning-powder|6]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Dokubo A, Dappa EN, Hamilton AA, Igwe FU · 2023</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/dokubo2023-nigeria-seasoning-powder">Heavy Metal Concentration and Human Health Risk Assessment of Selected Brands of Seasoning Powder Sold in Port Harcourt, Nigeria</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">International Journal of Science and Healthcare Research</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/dokubo2023-nigeria-seasoning-powder">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

<ul>
<li>
<strong>Metals measured:</strong>

Lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel and chromium and others, in 69 results from 7 sources.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Angelon-Gaetz KA, Klaus C, Chaudhry EA, Bean DK · 2018</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Lead in Spices, Herbal Remedies, and Ceremonial Powders Sampled from Home Investigations for Children with Elevated Blood Lead Levels — North Carolina, 2011–2018</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Stone A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Metals in Children&#39;s and Consumer Products and Packaging</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Washington State Department of Ecology, Hazardous Waste and Toxics Reduction Program, Publication 14-04-014 (Revised June 2021)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Main drivers:</strong>

Form, basis and where the flavoring was sampled; reporting limits decide how many results are non-detects.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Angelon-Gaetz KA, Klaus C, Chaudhry EA, Bean DK · 2018</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Lead in Spices, Herbal Remedies, and Ceremonial Powders Sampled from Home Investigations for Children with Elevated Blood Lead Levels — North Carolina, 2011–2018</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Stone A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Metals in Children&#39;s and Consumer Products and Packaging</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Washington State Department of Ecology, Hazardous Waste and Toxics Reduction Program, Publication 14-04-014 (Revised June 2021)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
<li>
<strong>Practical framing:</strong>

Ask for lot results with a quantification limit well below the limit that applies in the destination market.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Angelon-Gaetz KA, Klaus C, Chaudhry EA, Bean DK · 2018</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Lead in Spices, Herbal Remedies, and Ceremonial Powders Sampled from Home Investigations for Children with Elevated Blood Lead Levels — North Carolina, 2011–2018</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Stone A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Metals in Children&#39;s and Consumer Products and Packaging</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Washington State Department of Ecology, Hazardous Waste and Toxics Reduction Program, Publication 14-04-014 (Revised June 2021)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</li>
</ul>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the flavoring came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.



<a id="ingredient-identity-and-scope"></a>
## What counts as flavoring

<a id="hmi-ing-001"></a>
<a id="what-commodity-biological-species-edible-part-and-commercial-forms-does-this-page-cover"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-002"></a>
<a id="which-processing-states-and-derivatives-are-distinct-materials-rather-than-synonyms"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-003"></a>
<a id="what-evidence-can-be-transferred-between-those-materials-and-what-requires-a-separate-answer"></a>

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

15 rows can be rated against an HMI reference for the same form and basis, 0 rows have a direct same-study comparison, and the rest stay unrated with the reason recorded.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-flavoring" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-flavoring-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of flavoring, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>0.6–3 (bounded)</td><td>6–23.8 (bounded)</td><td>300</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>0.6–3 (bounded)</td><td>9.8–23.8 (bounded)</td><td>300</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>26 reference units; 5 sampling families. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>22</td><td>20%</td></tr><tr><td>Angelon-Gaetz et al. 2018</td><td>1</td><td>20%</td></tr><tr><td>Houlihan et al. 2019</td><td>1</td><td>20%</td></tr><tr><td>Stone 2021</td><td>1</td><td>20%</td></tr><tr><td>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2024</td><td>1</td><td>20%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for flavoring, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>5 sampling families with different designs; none is a probability sample of world supply. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li><li><a href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina" class="internal" data-slug="sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina" data-no-cite-meta>Angelon-Gaetz et al. 2018</a></li><li><a href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food" class="internal" data-slug="sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food" data-no-cite-meta>Houlihan et al. 2019</a></li><li><a href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products" class="internal" data-slug="sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products" data-no-cite-meta>Stone 2021</a></li><li><a href="../sources/fda2024-toxic-elements-baby-food-compliance-2009-2024" class="internal" data-slug="sources/fda2024-toxic-elements-baby-food-compliance-2009-2024" data-no-cite-meta>U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2024</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. GEMS/Food</a></p><p>Flavoring, as sold · Lead (Pb) · 3 results.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Selected finding and reference</p><div class="hmi-selected-result" data-selected-finding><strong class="hmi-finding">23 µg/kg (median)</strong><span class="hmi-row-note">median.</span><p>3 results. Basis: As sold, as eaten or fresh weight.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p></div><div class="hmi-selected-comparison" data-selected-comparison><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">HMI baseline position</p><p>Moderate · HMI flavoring Pb reference · Flavoring, as sold. median as reported: 23 µg/kg. HMI median 6–23.8 (bounded); Q1 0.6–3 (bounded); Q3 300 µg/kg. 5 families. Not a safety grade.</p><p>Low is below Q1; Moderate includes Q1 through Q3; High is above Q3. Reporting bounds must give the same category throughout.</p><p>This finding’s sampling family contributes to the fixed reference.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to weighting</p><p>Equal weight per subgroup: Moderate. The position is unchanged under this alternative. This check does not address all sampling, rounding or analytical uncertainty.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sensitivity to source selection</p><ul><li>Without WHO GEMS/Food: Moderate.</li><li>Without Angelon-Gaetz et al. 2018: Moderate.</li><li>Without Houlihan et al. 2019: Moderate.</li><li>Without Stone 2021: Moderate.</li><li>Without U.S. Food and Drug Administration 2024: Moderate.</li></ul><p>These checks show dependence on each sampling family; they are not confidence intervals.</p><div data-baseline-reference="hmi-pooled-reference-v2:flavoring-2753d3cb83ee:87b401b5bffd"><a href="#hmi-reference-hmi-pooled-reference-v2:flavoring-2753d3cb83ee:87b401b5bffd">Reference, contributing evidence and methods</a></div></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2018</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>AFRO</p><div data-selected-regional><p>Purchase or reporting market; growing area not identified.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>As described by the monitoring programme.</p><p>Non-detects are intervals from zero to the reporting limit; never a measured zero</p><p></p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>median</td><td>3 of 3 detected; HMI median of individual results</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>3 dataset rows, for example Lead.csv data row 160788.</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. <a id="trade-offs-and-failure-modes"></a>
## Trade-offs and limits of controls

<a id="hmi-ing-022"></a>
<a id="can-an-intervention-increase-another-contaminant-or-redistribute-contamination-to-a-by-product"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-023"></a>

<a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.

<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 1 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 4,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a <a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Flavoring FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-flavoring" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-flavoring" data-question="What heavy metals are found in flavoring?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in flavoring?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, total mercury, nickel, chromium, antimony, Co, Cu, Fe, Mn, Zn, V and Mo in flavoring, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Angelon-Gaetz KA, Klaus C, Chaudhry EA, Bean DK · 2018</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Lead in Spices, Herbal Remedies, and Ceremonial Powders Sampled from Home Investigations for Children with Elevated Blood Lead Levels — North Carolina, 2011–2018</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">MMWR Morbidity and Mortality Weekly Report</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/angelon-gaetz2018-lead-spices-north-carolina">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Jane Houlihan, Charlotte Brody · 2019</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">What&#39;s in My Baby&#39;s Food? A National Investigation Finds 95 Percent of Baby Foods Tested Contain Toxic Chemicals That Lower Babies&#39; IQ, Including Arsenic and Lead</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Healthy Babies Bright Futures</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/houlihan2019-hbbf-whats-in-baby-food">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Stone A · 2021</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Metals in Children&#39;s and Consumer Products and Packaging</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">Washington State Department of Ecology, Hazardous Waste and Toxics Reduction Program, Publication 14-04-014 (Revised June 2021)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/stone2021-wa-metals-childrens-consumer-products">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in flavoring, as sold from 74 results in 5 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 1.4 to 110 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-flavoring" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-flavoring" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in flavoring?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in flavoring?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, flavoring, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in PAHO (1.4 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (72.1 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in AFRO (3 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (48.1 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
